# Masi App — Build Log

> **This is the master build log.** One file, the whole history: what was built, when, why, what
> broke, what was decided, and what was verified. It absorbed `sqlite-refactor-log.md` on 2026-07-13.
>
> **Where to append:** new work goes in the **Verification Register** (section 1) as a dated row.
> Decisions that will outlive the task go in the **Decision Register** (section 2). Bugs and gaps
> found along the way go in the **Bug and Gap Register** (section 3).

## How this log works

- **Append-only, newest at the bottom of each register.** Do not rewrite history. If something turns
  out to be wrong, correct it with a NEW dated row that says so.
- **A row is only worth writing if a future reader would be worse off without it.** Record: what
  changed, the exact verification (command plus result, with suite and test counts), review findings
  and how they were engaged, device checks, and anything surprising. Assumptions and dead ends are
  worth as much as successes.
- **Update this log after every meaningful work session**, especially anything touching the database,
  sync, or RLS. This instruction lives in `AGENTS.md` too.
- Related standing docs: `documentation/rls-sync-contract-map.md` (the RLS/sync contract),
  `documentation/ROADMAP.md` (what is still open and why it is ordered that way),
  `documentation/device-gates-sqlite-backend-2026-07.md` (what Jim still needs to check on a device),
  and `PRD.md` (the current product and architecture contract).

### History structure

The record spans two eras with two different shapes, both preserved as written:

| Section | Era | Shape |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Verification Register | 2026-05-19 to present | Dated table. **The live append target.** |
| 2. Decision Register | 2026-05-20 onward | Dated table: decision, rationale, revisit trigger. |
| 3. Bug and Gap Register | SQLite refactor onward | Dated table of bugs and gaps found. |
| 4. Narrative Timeline | June 2026 (Top-10 tranche: Items 3, 4, 8, 5) | Prose entries. |
| 5. Archive | SQLite refactor working artifacts | Point-in-time checklists, kept for provenance. |

| 2026-07-14 | Domain-pull queue-granularity TDD tracers | RED reproduced at both child and class pull boundaries, then GREEN | Child RED event order was `rpc:start, unrelated, rpc:end, programme query`, proving the supplied queue was unused while the global queue held the whole pull. GREEN is `rpc:start, rpc:end, unrelated, programme query`: every dependent request has its own lease. Class RED observed one queue call for three requests; GREEN observes three. Two intermediate class GREEN attempts failed at compile time on local variable redeclarations and were corrected before any behavior test ran. |
| 2026-07-14 | Domain-pull queue fairness focused gate | Passed 9 suites / 101 tests on Node 20 | Covered child and class providers, real queue scheduling, cache-first contexts, auth cold-start/user switching, render isolation, pending deletes, and real-SQLite pull persistence/reconcile boundaries. All assertions passed. Established deliberate error-path logs, React/Paper warnings, and the existing open-handle notice remain non-failing output. |
| 2026-07-14 | Branch-wide SQLite integration gate after outbox queue stability and batch claim | Passed 31 suites / 276 tests on Node 20, exit 0 | Exact command: `PATH=$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v20.19.4/bin:$PATH npm run test:integration`. The real-SQLite manifest includes the queue-age and one-UPDATE/one-SELECT claim regressions plus outbox recovery, batching, force-stop, repository, context, and pull/reconcile paths. Established deliberate failure logs and the Jest open-handle notice remain non-failing output. |
| 2026-07-14 | Outbox queue-age TDD tracer | RED reproduced, then GREEN: 1 suite / 19 tests on Node 20 | The real-SQLite regression first showed re-enqueue changing `created_at` from `08:00` to `09:00`. `upsertRecord` now supports explicitly preserved conflict columns; outbox insert preserves `created_at` while refreshing every mutable field. Removing the now-redundant second UPDATE keeps enqueue to one write. Exact GREEN command: `PATH=$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v20.19.4/bin:$PATH npx jest __tests__/syncOutboxRepository.test.js --runInBand --silent`. |
| 2026-07-14 | Set-based batch-claim TDD tracer and adversarial gate | RED reproduced, then GREEN: 7 suites / 86 tests on Node 20 | The first RED had no `markInFlightAndGet`; the second proved `processBatch` still bypassed it. The repository now claims any bounded id set with one UPDATE and one SELECT, returns rows in input order with the fresh CAS timestamp, and the engine performs no per-row `getById` reads. The expanded gate covered batch success/failure, bulk finalize, auth switching, thrown local operations, deterministic error budgets, deferred fallback, and sibling-safe cleanup. Two failure-injection comments/conditions were updated because batch claim no longer calls the single-record `markInFlight` method. Exact GREEN command: `PATH=$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v20.19.4/bin:$PATH npx jest __tests__/syncOutboxRepository.test.js __tests__/offlineSyncOutbox.test.js __tests__/batchFailureSemantics.test.js __tests__/bulkFinalize.test.js __tests__/offlineSyncAuthGate.test.js __tests__/syncErrorGuard.test.js __tests__/deterministicErrorBudget.test.js --runInBand --silent`. |
| 2026-07-14 | Last-session letters product decision recorded | Decision complete; no runtime change | Jim chose the group-based interpretation. Session capture will become group-first, and "last session letters" will show the assigned group's latest literacy-session letters. The current child-first form will not receive a temporary per-child history summary. | 2026-06-01 | `npx jest __tests__/childClassReassignment.test.js -t "syncs cleanly"` | Failed as expected, then passed | #35 P1: red reproduced the offline create→reassign→sync seam by driving the real `createOutboxSyncEngine` against a second migrated SQLite db as the "server" (real NOT NULL + `idx_child_class_memberships_active_unique` enforced). Red failure was deterministic: the old membership's `archive` op fails `NOT NULL (child_id)` (nothing to archive remotely) and one `insert` dies on the active-membership UNIQUE index; which class survives is otherwise a UUID-sort coin-flip. Green: `updateChild` now detects a still-pending `insert` for the old membership and coalesces — rewriting that insert to carry `exited_at` instead of queuing a separate `archive`. A naive in-memory mock with merge semantics would have hidden the bug; the real-schema server was required to surface it. |

| 2026-06-03 | Field preview build surfaced a stuck `letter_mastery` sync failure | Root cause found | Exported logs show the same record (`ff17e146-...`) failing `2026-06-02` and again `2026-06-03` with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "idx_letter_mastery_unique_active"`; the Sync Status screen parks it in Failed Items with a Retry that re-fails. Root cause: `letter_mastery` is a logical-key-unique, push-only table that synced with a random per-device id (unlike `assessment_items`/`session_attendees`, which use deterministic ids), and `23505` is classified terminal. A fresh install over a backend that already held the EA's mastery (under old random ids) re-creates rows with new ids that collide on the logical-key index and stick forever. Decision (with Codex adversarial review): do NOT treat the duplicate as blind success (would leave local id ≠ server id and break later un-teach); instead canonicalize ids + adopt the server id. |
| 2026-06-03 | `npx jest __tests__/letterMasterySyncRepair.test.js` | Failed as expected, then passed | Red drove the real `createOutboxSyncEngine` against a second migrated DB as the server (real NOT NULL + `idx_letter_mastery_unique_active`), reproducing the exact `UNIQUE constraint failed: letter_mastery...` collision as a terminal failure. Green: on a `letter_mastery` `23505` the engine looks up the server's canonical id for the logical key, re-pushes under it, rewrites the local row + outbox, and marks synced. Guard test: a `23505` with no visible canonical row stays terminal (no blind success). |
| 2026-06-03 | `npx jest __tests__/masteryRepository.test.js __tests__/literacySessionPersistence.test.js` | Failed as expected, then passed | Added `letterMasteryDomainId` and switched `saveLetterMasteryRecord` to mint new rows with the deterministic logical-key id (caller-supplied random ids ignored for new rows; existing rows keep their id). Updated the rippled tests to assert by logical key rather than hard-coded ids — and to reflect that re-teaching a soft-deleted letter now reuses (un-deletes) the canonical row instead of creating a second history row. |
| 2026-06-03 | `npx jest --testPathIgnorePatterns '/node_modules/' '/\.claude/worktrees/'` | Passed | Full Jest gate passed 78 suites / 370 tests after the `letter_mastery` sync idempotency fix (deterministic ids + canonical-id adoption). |
| 2026-06-03 | Codex adversarial review of the letter_mastery fix | Two P1s found and addressed | P1-a: switching `saveLetterMasteryRecord` to deterministic ids discarded the caller-supplied id, so `LetterTrackerScreen` (which kept the old uuid in state) would no-op a toggle-off — letter silently stays mastered. Fix: `saveLetterMasteryRecord` returns the canonical id; `LetterTrackerScreen` + `literacySessionPersistence` store the returned id. P1-b: an `archive` op for a still-pending insert sorts ahead of it (id tie `archive` < `insert`), so a letter taught-then-untaught offline synced as stale-active (the #35 hazard, pre-existing in letter_mastery). Fix: repository-level coalescing — a soft-delete/edit on an unsynced row folds into the pending insert (`enqueueMasteryWrite`) instead of a separate op; adoption simplified accordingly. |
| 2026-06-03 | `npx jest --testPathIgnorePatterns '/node_modules/' '/\.claude/worktrees/'` | Passed | Full Jest gate passed 78 suites / 372 tests after addressing both Codex P1s. New coverage: `saveLetterMasteryRecord` returns the canonical id and a kept-old-id update no-ops (proving the caller hazard); a taught-then-untaught-offline letter coalesces to a single exited insert and syncs as not-active. |
| 2026-06-03 | Codex re-review (P1s confirmed resolved) | One P2 found and fixed | P2: a coalesced tombstone (taught-then-untaught offline) is a deleted-state insert; if the server holds the logical key active under a prior install's id, the tombstone upsert does NOT collide (the unique index ignores deleted rows), so the 23505 adoption never fired and the server stayed stale-active. This was the narrow limitation logged above. Fix: trigger adoption **proactively** for `letter_mastery` deleted-state inserts (before the upsert) — if an active server row under a different id exists, re-push the deleted state under it (archiving it) and adopt the id. The limitation is now closed. |
| 2026-06-03 | `npx jest --testPathIgnorePatterns '/node_modules/' '/\.claude/worktrees/'` | Passed | Full Jest gate passed 78 suites / 373 tests after the P2 fix. (One transient `CreateClassScreen` timeout flake under parallel load — passes in isolation, unrelated to letter_mastery; re-run was clean.) |
| 2026-06-04 | Architecture decision: drop runtime adoption for deterministic push ids | Pivot (user-approved) | After 4 Codex passes each surfaced a NEW concurrency edge in the adoption path (orphaned archive, cached-id staleness, tombstone non-collision, transient-lookup misclassification, in-flight race), the user and I concluded adoption was reconcile-after-divergence fighting the engine's concurrency model. Pivoted to prevention: deterministic logical-key ids everywhere (the codebase's existing `assessment_items`/`session_attendees` pattern). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-06-04-letter-mastery-deterministic-id-root-fix.md`. Codex plan-review hardened it into mandatory gates (cleanup sequencing, precise goal, mixed-version rollout constraint, a constraint-codifying test). |

Removed the entire adoption subsystem (`adoptLetterMasteryCanonicalId`, `fetchLetterMasteryCanonicalId`, `LETTER_MASTERY_LOGICAL_KEY`, both `processRecord` hooks) and its 4 tests. New tests: deterministic mapping, two-installs-converge idempotency, and a REGRESSION GUARD that codifies why legacy staging cleanup is mandatory (a deterministic push 23505-collides with a not-cleaned legacy random-id active row). | 2026-06-16 | Sync-reliability slice (Top-10 Items 1+2): writer/reader split + outbox convergence | Implementation complete & Codex-converged; release gate green; **device pass pending (user)** | 12 TDD tasks on `fix/sync-reliability-writer-batch`; full per-task detail in `documentation/archive/sync-reliability-build-log.md`. Dedicated persistent SQLite **writer** (WAL, busy_timeout, FK-on post-migration) + read-only **reader** (`query_only`) — kills the per-call `withExclusiveTransactionAsync` connection-storm + pragma leak; `withTransaction` non-re-entrant by contract; all writes audited onto the writer. CAS-preserving **bulk finalize** (1 txn / 200-chunk); **batch-failure** semantics (thrown/returned batch → per-record `allSettled` fallback, no double-send) + **per-record/preflight error guard** (no throw strands `in_flight` or aborts the pass; `result.preflightErrors` surfaced; stranded `in_flight` self-heals — counted toward auto-sync scheduling); 15-min backoff cap; manual **Sync-Now force-bypass** (coalesced across all affordances); 4 batchable tables; **contract-completeness** guards (column + PUSH_ORDER table-set). Every task adversarially Codex-reviewed to convergence (caught: rollback-masking, writer-bootstrap leak, fail-fast `Promise.all` double-send, syncAll preflight gap, in_flight-not-scheduled). | 2026-07-14 | Deploy gate: re-key pre-fix assignment ids on `masi-app-sqlite` (26 rows) | Applied and verified; 0 mismatches remain across all five tables; test-EA fixture intact (9 children, 1 class, 7 groups) | Method: recompute each row's correct deterministic id **in SQL** (`uuid_generate_v5`, namespace `09dcf4b2-...`, parts joined by `chr(31)` — mirroring `deterministicDomainId`) and diff against the stored id. `letter_mastery` was **already clean** (13/13 current-formula v5), so that gate needed nothing. The other four tables held 26 landmines: `child_ea_assignments` 9, `child_programme_enrollments` 9, `class_ea_assignments` 1, `group_ea_assignments` 7. **The gate's original wording was wrong and would have caused a miss:** it said clean *"random-id"* rows, but all 7 `group_ea_assignments` rows carried valid **v5 deterministic** ids generated by a **superseded formula** (`group_id, ea_user_id, programme_id`; current `groupEaAssignmentDomainId` keys on `group_id` alone — confirmed by matching all 7 against the old formula). A cleanup filtering on "random ids" would have skipped every one and left the 23505 collision live. **The only sound test is whether the stored id equals what today's code computes for that row's logical key**, not whether the id looks random — with deterministic ids the derivation is part of the data contract. Fixed by re-key rather than wipe, deliberately: there is no seed script (the 2026-cohort script is unbuilt and `seed_data_plan.md` is schema-dead), so a wipe would have blocked the device gates behind building one. `child_programme_enrollments` has no identity trigger so a plain UPDATE sufficed; the other three have `prevent_assignment_identity_change`, which raises on `new.id is distinct from old.id` but fires on UPDATE only, so they were delete+reinserted inside one transaction preserving every other column (zero inbound FK references, verified). |

| 2026-07-13 | WelaPLUS worktree merge-debt assessment (trial merge, throwaway worktree) | Merge is clean: 1 doc conflict; merged tree 201 suites / 1233 tests green. **Verdict: do not merge as-is.** | The battery is an unwired island (5,236 LOC, 28 test files, nothing in `src/` imports it) on a branch 310 commits behind main. 310 commits of drift cost one line of `CLAUDE.md` — the island architecture worked. **But the green suite is the trap.** 🔴 The worktree rewrites `assessmentItemDomainId` in `domainRepositoryUtils.js` to include `itemKey` when `position` is present; main hashes positioned rows on position alone. **That is live shipping code** — `assessmentsRepository.js:217,226,240` passes `position: item.index` for every EGRA letter item, and `offlineSync.js:464` is the push path. Merging silently rekeys every positioned `assessment_items` row: re-saves insert duplicates beside the originals and the outbox upserts by the new id, double-counting server-side. It does not crash and does not fail a build. **Every guard is structurally blind:** `offlineSync.stripping.test.js:136` asserts `payload.id === assessmentItemDomainId(...)`, recomputing the expectation with the function under change — it asserts `f(x) == f(x)` and can never fail; the `syncErrorGuard` tests only assert uniqueness, which the new shape satisfies. The change is arguably correct (fixes a real Q11 `ea:`/`hq:` rubric collision) but must land as its own PR with a contract-map update, a staging `assessment_items` wipe, and a test pinning the literal expected UUID — while `masi-app-sqlite` still has no field users. Also merge-clean-but-wrong: 52 hardcoded navy/green hexes across the 11 Question components (the colour guard validates `Object.values(colors)`, i.e. the token file, **not the codebase** — the missing guard is a raw-hex-literal grep), duplicated chrome vs the new `BottomSheet`/`CaptureHeader` primitives, and a `tsc` gate added to `test:release` without `typescript` installed. Good news: zero worktree migrations, so no v5/v6/v7 collision — main is at `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 7` and WelaPLUS starts cleanly at v8. Recommended: restore `domainRepositoryUtils.js` from main on the branch, then merge (~1h); land the rekey deliberately (~half a day). Detail in `documentation/ROADMAP.md` §7. |
| 2026-07-13 | Design foundation sprint: shared BottomSheet, ten picker conversions, capture chrome, roster virtualization (audit #8, roadmap item 12) | Built with strict TDD; unit `172/1031` and integration `30/257` green | One `BottomSheet` primitive replaced four drifted copies of the modal scaffolding (backdrop 0.5 vs 0.4 opacity, 36x4 vs 40x4 handle, token vs hardcoded radius); a `SelectSheet` on top of it converted ten picker instances (nine Paper Dialogs plus an inline sheet nobody had inventoried in EditChildScreen). `ClockInBeforeSessionDialog` deliberately stays a Dialog: it is a three-way decision, not a picker. Capture chrome extracted (`AssessmentInstructions`, `CaptureHeader`, `EndAssessmentButton`), single-sourcing the End-Assessment copy. Roster fix (audit #8): the two adversarial reviews KILLED the first design twice. (1) A bounded FlatList inside the form's ScrollView is the pattern React Native explicitly warns against, and with the default windowSize of 21 a 60-child roster stays entirely in the render window, so it would have virtualized nothing. Fix: the form's root scroller BECOMES the roster list, with header/footer components. (2) Keying `onToggle` on the selection Set gives it a new identity per selection, so every memoized row re-renders and the one-row assertion was unreachable. Fix: ref-backed, permanently-stable `onToggle` plus `extraData`. (3) A render-local ListHeaderComponent is a NEW component type every keystroke, which remounts the subtree and drops focus from the comment TextInput mid-typing; header/footer must be hoisted, stable types. RED evidence: one tap re-rendered 10 rows and every keystroke re-rendered 10 rows; now 1 and 0. Twelve binding review dispositions (R1-R12). NOT proven by tests: native windowing, which needs Jim's low-end Android gate. Merged `3db4c96`. |

No app source, installed build, OTA, device, or field behavior changed. |

This was documentation/provenance work only after the hosted migration gate; no app test, build, deployment, device, or field claim is added. |

PostgreSQL re-proved all six actor classes, complete-family visibility, same-connection actor switching, owner paging, microsecond cursors, same-tuple exhaustion, and the RPC matrix. Fix: reject query keys outside `sslmode`, `connect_timeout`, and `application_name`, naming the offending key; export a component-only URL builder used for both targets (all queries/fragments omitted); export and use an environment builder removing all nine specified routing variables while preserving `PGPASSWORD`, `PGPASSFILE`, and per-call `PGAPPNAME`. ## Decision Register

| Date | Decision | Rationale | Revisit Trigger |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2026-09-04 | Treat 1,000 rows as the verified hosted PostgREST response cap and require explicit bounded pagination, deadlines, and completeness accounting in the first implementation of every potentially larger pull family. | A real cap-plus-one request returned exactly 1,000 of 1,205 rows with HTTP 206 and an exact content range. A successful but capped response is incomplete replication, not a performance inconvenience; the current assessment-item estate is already close enough that a non-paginated intermediate contract would be throwaway and unsafe. | The hosted API cap is deliberately reconfigured and re-proven through an equivalent cap-plus-one probe, or a family moves behind a server operation whose response-completeness contract is independently bounded and tested. |
| 2026-08-29 | Treat an authorized session as one complete delivery-history aggregate. Owning the session or having a historical direct delivery assignment to any attendee grants the complete parent and every attendee row. | The parent activities JSON already carries child-keyed facts for the delivery event, and a child-filtered attendee list would imply a privacy boundary that does not exist while also misrepresenting the event. Admission to the session is the authorization boundary. | Masi introduces a less-trusted reader class or genuinely sensitive per-child facts. Put such facts behind a separately authorized table or redacted projection rather than silently adding them to this aggregate. |
| 2026-08-27 | Correct and prove activity-specific history authorization before hydration. Build bounded keyset pagination and structural request deadlines into the first session/attendee slice, then reuse the completeness contract for assessments/items. Do not port Zazi protocol v2 wholesale; require any second writer, shared mutable aggregate, side-effecting RPC, or partial-unsafe family to prove stable-id upsert is sufficient or adopt a narrower causal/idempotent command. | Hydration makes server-visible rows durable offline, so an over-broad RLS predicate becomes a data-distribution bug. A server row cap and a hung request are correctness boundaries, not later performance enhancements; the live estate already contains 900 items and one actor sees 604. Stable ids solve duplicate identity but do not by themselves order competing writers or make side effects idempotent. | Authenticated PostgreSQL tests prove the accepted scope, cap-plus-one fixtures and hung-request tests prove completeness/failure semantics, or the aggregate/writer model changes. |
| 2026-08-27 | Derive session-history parent pages from positive owner/direct-delivery grants in a least-privilege RPC, then keyset-page the authorized set. Keep raw table RLS correct for point reads/upsert visibility, but do not make a Programme-wide RLS scan the hydration API. | Correct row policies still tested every Programme candidate. On the local hostile fixture the raw plan visibly filtered 2,003 candidates and its root reported 14,311 shared blocks, while the positive-authority RPC root reported 803. The opaque function plan does not prove an inner index choice; the authorization relationship—not a generic ordering index—is still the selective design predicate. | Hosted plans show a materially different optimizer shape; delivery authority gains another valid grant; or a future server API returns complete parent/attendee families atomically with equivalent least privilege. |
| 2026-08-27 | Use the no-active-user window for a source-grounded pre-live hardening programme. Treat Zazi as a field-tested reference, not a code donor: share portfolio invariants and conformance attacks first; preserve Masi's writer and reconcile safety; implement sessions then assessments hydration; add minimum incident/release provenance in parallel; extract packages only after two shipped consumers prove an identical responsibility. | Structural changes are cheapest before trusted work accumulates on phones, but Masi and Zazi differ materially in Programme, identity, grouping, authorization, aggregate, and backend contracts. The common value is concentrated in failure models, protocol guarantees, evidence gates, and operational practices rather than byte-identical code. | Gate 0 finds active Masi users/data or an installed-estate compatibility constraint; two implementations prove a stable runtime seam; or field evidence shows a different slice is the highest data-safety risk. |
| 2026-07-23 | Keep stable product requirements in `PRD.md`, all current priority/open-work status in `documentation/ROADMAP.md`, execution-sized tasks in GitHub issues, and implementation evidence in `documentation/build-log.md`. Route agents through `docs/agent-context/` before human-facing detail. | Stable requirements, fast-moving status, executable tickets, and historical evidence change at different rates. Combining them created duplicate ledgers and stale claims. Progressive disclosure gives agents maximum relevant context without forcing every task to ingest the full human documentation tree. | The repository adopts a generated documentation system or issue tracker integration that can prove one canonical source without hand-maintained duplication. |
| 2026-07-23 | Make bidirectional session and assessment history convergence the next sync slice after current pilot activation/device checks, ahead of cosmetic improvements. | Supabase already holds the data, but a fresh TestFlight install cannot recover it because `sessions`/`session_attendees` and `assessments`/`assessment_items` are outbound-only. A server that accepts uploads without repopulating a fresh device is not yet a continuity boundary for reinstall, replacement, second-device, or seeded history. | Both parent-child families hydrate transactionally and pass first-install, reinstall, second-device, offline-restart, collision-rail, completeness, and physical-device gates. |
| 2026-07-23 | Until the app implements formal Assessment Windows, use complete locally available active-roster lifetime coverage on Home and Assessments, and all locally available records in Assessment History. Treat group picking as a shortcut that resolves to child ids, not as group-first persistence. | The former 30-day History and current-month Home cutoffs described different products and hid valid local records. The current assessment write contract persists children, not a durable assessment-window or selected-group identity, so presenting either concept as authoritative would overstate the domain model. | Assessment Windows become part of the app's query contract, or session persistence gains the planned group-first contract. |
| 2026-07-22 | Pull persistence supersedes a stale same-pair `synced` active row instead of wedging on the partial unique index; non-`synced` collisions skip the incoming row. | Deterministic active-pair ids remain the primary convergence contract, but id-contract violations (server re-keys, superseded formulas, bad seeds) are now survivable: an offline field device heals on its next pull instead of erroring forever with no remote fix possible. Mirrors reconcile's rails exactly, so pending local work is never destroyed by a pull. | A push-side equivalent is designed (the §0b seed wedge has no rail), or the deterministic-id scheme itself is replaced. |
| 2026-07-22 | Treat the South African programme day as the single session-capture and dashboard date; treat outbox `archive` as an acknowledged lifecycle UPDATE, never an UPSERT. | Write/read date disagreement produced correctly saved but apparently missing sessions for an overseas tester. Archive upsert invoked INSERT RLS against intentionally partial payloads; direct UPDATE matches the domain transition, while lifecycle allowlists and returned-id acknowledgment prevent identity rewrite and false-green sync. | Masi operates across multiple programme timezones, or archive transitions move behind an atomic server RPC with its own receipt contract. |
| 2026-07-22 | Render every bottom sheet through react-native-paper `Portal` with flexbox bottom anchoring; do not use React Native's `Modal` (or absolute `bottom: 0` inside an overlay) for sheets. | Measured on the iOS 26.1 simulator under the new architecture: transparent `Modal` parked correctly-laid-out sheet content a full window-height above the viewport regardless of content, animation type, or keyboard-avoidance state, and absolute bottom anchoring failed identically inside a Portal. Portal + `justifyContent: flex-end` rendered correctly and was device-verified. Portal keeps sheets in the ordinary view hierarchy the rest of the app already proves reliable. | React Native fixes transparent-Modal positioning under Fabric and the project verifies it on both platforms, or a sheet is needed outside the `PaperProvider` subtree. |
| 2026-07-21 | Ship Sentry as allowlisted operational telemetry, keep detailed console logs local, and disable Replay/visual/default-PII capture for the initial field release. | The app handles child, staff, assessment, attendance, and location data. Structured crash and sync context provides the release-critical signal without continuously uploading screen content or arbitrary console arguments that may contain domain payloads. | A documented privacy review, retention decision, and physical-device redaction test on both platforms establishes that a specific additional capture source is necessary and safe. |
| 2026-07-21 | Advance the first Sentry-native pilot to app/runtime 1.3.0 and build it from a release branch. | `@sentry/react-native` changes the native binary. Reusing runtime 1.2.0 could let an OTA bundle that assumes Sentry native code reach older 1.2.0 binaries without that code. The app-version runtime policy makes the minor version bump the compatibility boundary. | The project adopts fingerprint-based runtime versions with an equally strict native compatibility gate. |
| 2026-07-21 | Provision pilot onboarding testers through a narrow SQLite-only capability, not the general cohort importer. | Pilot accounts need Auth, normalized profile, school, job title, and active Programme assignment. They do not justify inventing Head Office identity and roster mappings. Restricting the tool to explicit zero-class testers prevents accidental legacy writes and avoids a second partial roster importer. | Jim supplies and maps the real Head Office source model, or the pilot requires additional seeded accounts beyond the existing seeded EA. |
| 2026-07-14 | Make session capture group-first and derive "last session letters" from the group's latest literacy session. | One session is one group-block, `sessions.group_id` is the durable historical context, and per-child latest sessions can disagree inside one selection. A temporary child-by-child fold-in would encode the wrong unit of work and be discarded by the group-centred rebuild. | Masi changes the settled rule that one session represents one group-block of work. |
| 2026-05-20 | Use a clean-slate SQLite cutover instead of migrating local AsyncStorage domain data. | Field users can install a fresh app and restart from the new backend, which removes the riskiest compatibility work. | User decides current field-test device data must be preserved. |
| 2026-05-20 | Split the old monolithic plan into one spec plus six smaller implementation plans. | Zazi's refactor found many late review issues; smaller reviewable plans reduce conflict and make logs more useful. | A plan grows across unrelated ownership boundaries. |
| 2026-05-20 | Keep npm for this refactor. | The repo currently uses npm and package-lock; changing package managers during storage replacement adds avoidable risk. | User explicitly asks for pnpm migration or dependency security work becomes the main task. |
| 2026-05-20 | Separate job titles from programmes. | Job titles are profile/HR labels; programmes are operational dimensions for groups, sessions, assessments, and child work. | A staff member can hold multiple active programme assignments at the same time. |
| 2026-05-20 | Use `child_ea_assignments` and `child_group_memberships` in the clean schema. | The new backend is not constrained by old table names, and the EA wording matches the operational handoff model better than generic staff wording. | Masi decides non-EA staff roles must use the same table name. |
| 2026-05-20 | Migrate `app.json` to `app.config.js` in Plan 1. | Supabase target guardrails need dynamic config; treating this as optional leaves the old EAS `.env` pitfall in place. | Expo config needs to stay static for an EAS constraint we uncover. |
| 2026-05-20 | Auto-enroll newly created children into the actor's current programme. | Child creation should produce a complete working record without requiring a separate programme enrollment step. | EAs are allowed to create children for a programme other than their active assignment. |
| 2026-05-20 | Allow children to have multiple concurrent programme enrollments. | The same child may receive literacy, numeracy, and other support from different EAs; programme belongs to the work relationship, not permanently to the child. | Masi decides a child can belong to only one programme at a time. |
| 2026-05-20 | Scope My Children to the user's active programme. | EAs work one programme at a time, so the working list should show children assigned to the user and enrolled in that current programme. | Product needs a default cross-programme child list. |
| 2026-05-20 | Permit broad cross-programme reads for the same child at RLS level and enforce programme display in repositories. | Trusted field staff may need handover/history visibility, and simpler RLS is easier to debug than programme-isolated policies. | Masi adds less-trusted partner, parent, or external read-only users. |
| 2026-05-20 | Use dual SELECT policies for mobile-created tables. | Assignment/membership SELECT handles normal visibility; `created_by = auth.uid()` preserves upsert visibility before related join rows sync. | Backend no longer uses Supabase upserts from the mobile client. |
| 2026-05-20 | Enforce handover write blocking through active `child_ea_assignments`. | Historical assignees can read prior work, but old EAs should not keep writing child-specific event rows after handover. | Product wants old EAs to keep contributing after handover. |
| 2026-05-20 | Keep `time_entries` self-scoped by `user_id`. | Time tracking belongs to the staff member, not to child assignment state. | Time entries become manager-editable through the mobile app. |
| 2026-05-20 | Use one-shot field cutover with user-managed 1-2 day notice. | The user accepts losing unsynced device data at cutover and prefers a clean simultaneous restart. | Field operations require a rolling or reversible cutover. |
| 2026-05-20 | Keep `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` aligned with the clean-slate refactor before implementation starts. | Future agents should not optimize for legacy backwards compatibility or skip the refactor log/TDD discipline after the user locked the clean-slate decision. | The refactor finishes or the user changes the cutover strategy. |
| 2026-05-21 | Treat `masi-app-sqlite` as the clean-slate backend intended to become primary after validation. | The user created the new Supabase project and left current `masi-app` intact, matching the clean-slate refactor plan. | User decides to keep `masi-app-sqlite` staging-only and create a separate production backend later. |
| 2026-05-21 | Add `academic_years` as a first-class table; classes become year-specific via `academic_year_id`. | User confirmed multi-year operation; without academic years, year-over-year reporting collides on class name. | Masi pivots to single-year operation. |
| 2026-05-21 | Use a hard-fail invariant for multiple active academic years. | The partial unique index keeps year activation deliberate and simple; the active 2026 seed supplies the required current year. | Admin workflow needs one migration to activate a new year and deactivate the old one atomically. |
| 2026-05-21 | Add `assessment_windows` with baseline/midline/endline; tie official assessments to a window and leave ad-hoc progress checks windowless. | Formal assessment campaigns are part of Masi reporting; date-range inference is too brittle. | Masi drops formal assessment windows. |
| 2026-05-21 | Add `child_class_memberships` with academic year; `children.class_id` becomes a current/compatibility pointer. | Children can change classes mid-year, and old work must stay linked to the old class for reports. | Masi decides children never change classes mid-year. |
| 2026-05-21 | Add `teachers` table without teacher assignment history tables. | Reporting on teachers is not planned now but might come later; a first-class current teacher table is cheap to add now and expensive to retrofit. | Reporting needs teacher-level history; add `teacher_school_assignments` and `class_teacher_assignments` then. |
| 2026-05-21 | Add `class_ea_assignments` and `group_ea_assignments`; EAs can be assigned at child, class, or group level. | User confirmed Masi uses a mix of all three assignment levels in practice. | Masi simplifies to a single assignment level. |
| 2026-05-21 | Add `grouping_versions` and `class_grouping_state` for versioned grouping. | EAs regroup mid-year and prior groupings must remain as history. | Masi decides regrouping is rare enough to do in place without versioning. |
| 2026-05-21 | Skip Zazi tables `education_assistants`, `child_school_enrollments`, `staff_identity_links`, external ID bridges, `teacher_school_assignments`, and `class_teacher_assignments`. | Each can be added later if the operational model needs it; carrying them now adds RLS surface without clear value. | Admin/PM features need a separate EA roster, child mobility between schools becomes common, or teacher history becomes reportable. |
| 2026-05-21 | Write policy for child-specific event rows uses an OR across direct child assignment, class assignment plus active class membership, and group assignment plus active group membership. | EAs can earn write access through any of the three assignment paths; all paths must include both active assignment and active membership filters to prevent stale-assignment writes. | A new assignment path is added, or the multi-path OR becomes too expensive per row. |
| 2026-05-21 | Disable direct mobile hard delete on `children`; use `delete_child_if_no_history(child_id)` for the rare no-history delete path. | Child event rows have cascading FKs, so direct delete could silently erase assessment, mastery, and attendance history. Children with history should be archived or unassigned instead. | Product introduces a server-side admin console with explicit audited delete approval. |
| 2026-05-21 | Archive repositories must end active assignments/enrollments in the same transaction as the archive marker. | Archived children/classes/groups should not stay active in My Children, class, group, or assignment reports. | Archive semantics change to mean display-only hidden state. |
| 2026-05-21 | Admin-precreated classes are supported; EAs may self-assign only when the class is visible through their active school/programme assignment. | Masi will have admin-created/prepopulated classes; requiring `classes.created_by = auth.uid()` would block legitimate mobile claiming. | Admin workflows move all class assignment through service-role only. |
| 2026-05-21 | Future active-year rollover is a deliberate migration transaction, not automatic year switching. | The active-year unique index hard-fails multiple active years; future maintainers need to deactivate the old year and activate the new year atomically. | Masi wants an admin UI to change active years interactively. |
| 2026-05-21 | Make the SQLite staging launcher responsible for local Android SDK discovery and a fixed Metro port. | Codex can use the user's Android emulator when the project launcher injects `ANDROID_HOME`, `ANDROID_SDK_ROOT`, and SDK tool paths; pinning the staging Metro port avoids non-interactive Expo prompts when another Metro server already owns 8081. | The repo moves to a managed dev-client workflow with a different launch path. |
| 2026-05-21 | Use normalized SQLite repositories as the Plan 3 domain boundary and keep `src/utils/storage.js` as a temporary facade. | Repositories now own SQL, transaction boundaries, outbox enqueue, and normalized table contracts while current contexts/screens continue using named storage methods. | Plan 5 finishes migrating contexts/screens directly to repositories and the facade can be removed. |
| 2026-05-21 | Preserve current screen-shaped payloads temporarily in `local_state` sidecars while SQLite stores normalized rows. | Existing screens still expect AsyncStorage-style objects such as session `children_ids` and assessment letter arrays; the sidecar prevents a UI rewrite inside Plan 3 while normalized rows become the source for sync. | Plan 5 removes remaining screen-level storage callers and reads normalized repository view models directly. |
| 2026-05-21 | Treat child creation as four domain writes when class is known: child, EA assignment, programme enrollment, and class membership. | The schema has four durable rows for that action, so Plan 3 enqueues four matching outbox items rather than inventing a fifth side-effect row. | A later sync contract adds an explicit audit/event row for child creation. |
| 2026-05-21 | Relationship rows created by child save use fresh UUIDs plus active-row lookups for idempotency. | Deterministic child/user/programme/class IDs erase handover history when an ended relationship later becomes active again; active unique indexes already enforce single active rows. | A future sync model requires deterministic client-generated IDs for conflict-free relationship creation. |
| 2026-05-21 | `deleteClass` and `deleteGroup` are archive aliases in the storage facade layer. | Mobile hard delete without a synced outbox signal creates server orphans; archive preserves history and keeps a syncable mutation. | Product explicitly adds no-history hard-delete flows for classes or groups. |
| 2026-05-21 | Local-only legacy session metadata stays in SQLite but is stripped from session outbox payloads. | Current screens need legacy fields until Plan 5, but Supabase should not receive `__legacySession` implementation details as opaque session activity data. | Plan 5 removes legacy session fields from the repository shape entirely. |
| 2026-05-21 | Treat `sync_outbox` as the only Plan 4 push source. | Scanning domain rows by `sync_status` repeats AsyncStorage-era failure modes; repositories already enqueue durable row-level operations. | Plan 4 code review finds a required sync operation that cannot be represented as an outbox row. |
| 2026-05-21 | Delete `repairOrphanedJunctions` instead of porting it. | It repaired a historical AsyncStorage/junction bug and is not applicable to the clean-slate normalized SQLite schema. | Field telemetry from the SQLite build proves a new orphan pattern that needs a different repair. |
| 2026-05-21 | Treat `23505` duplicate-key responses as success only for explicitly configured tables. | Global duplicate-as-success can hide genuine partial-unique conflicts; success must be an intentional table-level decision. | A future server RPC makes duplicate handling idempotent for all mobile writes. |
| 2026-05-21 | Make local legacy programme references terminal sync failures. | The temporary `local-legacy-programme` row is deliberately terminal/local-only; records that depend on it should stay visible as unsyncable instead of trying to push invalid foreign keys. | Plan 5 removes the storage facade paths that can create legacy programme fallback records. |
| 2026-05-21 | Build Supabase payloads from table-specific server-column allowlists. | Sidecar compatibility fields such as `children_ids`, `group_ids`, and EGRA summary arrays must not leak from local view models into normalized Supabase tables. | Server schema intentionally adds a new mobile-writable column; add it to the allowlist and tests. |
| 2026-05-21 | Split `OfflineContext` into non-blocking `triggerBackgroundSync()` and shared-promise `syncNow()`. | Write paths need to refresh local status immediately without waiting for upload, while manual sync callers should wait for the same active sync instead of launching duplicates. | Product needs a blocking write-and-sync flow for a specific workflow. |
| 2026-05-21 | Time entry repository writes must enqueue `sync_outbox` rows in the same SQLite transaction as the domain row. | Clock-in/out is a first-class offline write; relying on later `sync_status` scans would recreate the AsyncStorage-era gap Plan 4 is removing. | Time entries move to a server RPC that owns both domain mutation and queueing. |
| 2026-05-21 | `syncAll()` resets stale `in_flight` outbox rows to `pending` at the start of each cycle. | A mobile process can die after marking a row in flight; restart must retry instead of stranding the row forever. | The engine gains a durable lease/heartbeat model with expiry windows. |
| 2026-05-21 | Outbox finalization uses compare-and-swap on `sync_outbox.id`, `updated_at`, and `status = 'in_flight'`. | If a user edits a row while an older payload is uploading, the older finalize must not delete the newer outbox payload or mark the domain row synced. | Sync processing becomes single-threaded across process lifetime by a stronger platform lock. |
| 2026-05-21 | Production duplicate-key handling remains terminal by default. | With `upsert(..., onConflict: 'id')`, `23505` usually means a different unique constraint was violated and should stay visible for review. | A table-specific duplicate can be proven safe and added to `safeDuplicateSuccessTables`. |
| 2026-05-21 | Plan 4 keeps `pullReferenceData` as an exported full-replace helper; production wiring is deferred to Plan 5. | Plan 4 built and tested the cache refresh primitive, but context hydration belongs with the Plan 5 screen/context migration. | First authenticated SQLite sign-in needs reference caches before Plan 5 starts. |
| 2026-05-21 | Domain outbox enqueue resets retry metadata whenever a user write refreshes the payload. | A user correction should retry immediately and should not inherit an old `next_retry_at` or stale error from the payload it replaced. | The sync engine moves retry state to immutable attempt rows instead of the active outbox row. |
| 2026-05-21 | Unknown sync tables terminalize the outbox row without updating a domain table. | Malformed or future-table rows should be visible failed items, not stuck pending rows or crashes from updating a table that does not exist locally. | Dynamic table registration is added to the sync engine. |
| 2026-05-21 | Auth profile/preload reads are deferred out of `onAuthStateChange` and serialized through a Supabase request queue. | Supabase warns against awaiting database calls inside auth callbacks; serial preload requests also reduce cold-start request races. | Supabase client behavior changes or a dedicated backend bootstrap RPC replaces client-side profile reads. |
| 2026-05-21 | ChildrenContext hydrates children, groups, and child-group memberships through one cache-first preload path. | Zazi hit duplicate pull races when providers performed separate server pulls; one bundle distributes results to all setters and preserves cache on partial errors. | Plan 5 introduces a richer domain pull engine that owns children/classes/groups hydration. |
| 2026-05-21 | Class creation uses the active `academic_years` row automatically. | Classes are year-specific in the SQLite schema; relying on screens to pass the year would create inconsistent class rows. | Masi allows users to create future-year classes from a picker. |
| 2026-05-21 | Class archive/delete contexts delegate child side effects to repository/storage transactions. | Context-level child nulling double-wrote child rows and could fight repository archive semantics. | Repository archive semantics are removed and class deletion becomes a purely visual filter. |
| 2026-05-21 | Context server pulls save rows through typed repository-backed storage methods instead of generic `storage.setItem(STORAGE_KEYS...)` cache replacement. | Generic list replacement bypasses normalized repository behavior and can recreate the AsyncStorage-era cache wipe pattern Plan 5 is removing. | A future pull engine owns full domain replacement atomically across related tables. |
| 2026-05-21 | Keep the remaining direct `AsyncStorage` usage centralized in `src/utils/storage.js` until Plan 6. | Plan 5 removed screen/context/component generic storage callers; removing the whole storage facade/profile fallback in the same slice would enlarge the auth startup blast radius. | Plan 6 starts cleanup/export/release gate work. |
| 2026-05-21 | Delete `asyncStorageSanitizer` and `SchemaHardeningBootstrap` instead of porting them. | They existed to repair legacy AsyncStorage data shapes; clean-slate SQLite installs do not need a compatibility sanitizer running at startup. | Product decides to support in-place upgrades from legacy AsyncStorage builds. |
| 2026-05-21 | Default user-facing repository reads are programme-scoped when called with `userId`. | My Children, sessions, assessments, letter mastery, groups, classes, stats, and rankings should show the actor's current programme slice by default. Cross-programme history remains an explicit future view, not the standard field workflow. | Product adds a cross-programme history/admin toggle. |
| 2026-05-21 | User-facing domain writes fail fast when no active programme assignment exists. | Clean-slate builds should not create `local-legacy-programme` rows from sessions, assessments, mastery, or groups; a missing assignment is a setup/data problem that should remain visible. | Product needs offline draft creation before programme assignment is available. |
| 2026-05-21 | UI child creation uses the atomic repository path. | Add Child must create child, EA assignment, programme enrollment, and class membership in one local transaction instead of saving a child row plus a legacy staff-child row. | Child creation moves to a backend RPC. |
| 2026-05-21 | Align Masi's Supabase client to Zazi's `@supabase/supabase-js@2.100.1` during Plan 5 Android validation. | The older 2.91.0 auth lock implementation can emit delayed lock-timeout warnings because it does not clear successful acquire timers; Zazi is already on the patched client family. | Supabase auth client changes regress React Native process-lock behavior. |
| 2026-05-21 | Use one singleton Supabase client/AppState listener across module reloads. | Expo/Fast Refresh and Android restarts should not create duplicate clients, refresh tickers, or AppState listeners against the same persisted auth storage key. | App moves to a dev-client/runtime where module reload behavior is proven different. |
| 2026-05-21 | Auth startup relies on `onAuthStateChange`'s `INITIAL_SESSION` event instead of also calling `getSession()`. | `getSession()` plus `INITIAL_SESSION` creates duplicate startup lock contenders; one official initial-session path is enough and is easier to reason about. | Supabase removes or changes the initial-session subscription behavior. |
| 2026-05-22 | Authenticated startup must pull reference data before publishing `user` to child providers. | `staff_programme_assignments`, schools, programmes, academic years, windows, and teachers are producer data for programme-scoped reads/writes; publishing `user` first lets contexts hydrate against an impossible empty local reference world. | A backend bootstrap RPC hydrates all reference data and profile state atomically. |
| 2026-05-22 | Reference data pulls upsert dependency-ordered rows instead of deleting whole referenced tables. | Deleting academic years, schools, or programmes during a pull can violate local FKs once classes/children exist; cache refresh should update server rows without breaking local history. | A pull engine gains audited tombstones or safe pruning for unreferenced reference rows. |
| 2026-05-22 | Successful context pulls keep only dirty absent local rows. | If the server no longer returns a synced child/group/class row, the working list should reflect cross-device archive/unassignment; unsynced, failed, or in-flight local rows are still preserved. | Relationship-level pull state replaces list-level merge logic. |
| 2026-05-22 | Completed assessment and letter-tracker writes need controlled error states and retry paths. | A repository write failure should not navigate away or show a false UI success; field users need a visible retry without losing completed work. | These flows move to a shared local-write command component with standard error handling. |
| 2026-05-22 | History screens refresh on focus and dashboard time stats are user-scoped. | Programme/user context can change while tab screens stay mounted; focused screens should reload local repository state, and days-worked must not count another signed-in user's local cache. | App introduces a global active-programme switch with explicit history cache invalidation. |
| 2026-05-22 | Mobile-created classes create a `class_ea_assignments` row in the same transaction. | User-scoped class reads filter through active class EA assignments; without the producer row, newly-created classes become locally invisible after refresh and cannot reliably authorize class-level work. | Admin/service-role class assignment becomes the only class assignment path. |
| 2026-05-22 | `session_attendees` and `assessment_items` use deterministic UUID ids instead of composite local ids. | The server schema has UUID primary keys; deterministic UUIDs preserve idempotent outbox upserts without pushing invalid composite ids or creating duplicates on retry. | Server tables gain natural-key unique constraints plus RPC-owned idempotency. |
| 2026-05-22 | Session SELECT policy keeps direct owner visibility for Supabase upserts. | PostgREST upsert requires SELECT visibility; newly-created session parents have no attendees yet, so owner visibility cannot depend only on attendee-derived helpers. | Session creation moves to a backend RPC that inserts parent and attendees atomically. |
| 2026-05-22 | Reference-data pulls share the global SQLite write queue. | Android cold-start showed `database is locked` when startup reference writes and sync finalization overlapped; every default write path must serialize through the same queue. | SQLite client changes to a native single-writer queue with stronger guarantees. |
| 2026-05-22 | Server-pulled assignment and membership junction rows are first-class offline cache data. | Programme-scoped reads inner-join relationship tables; handover/admin-assigned data must persist the same junction rows locally or it only works while online. | A backend bootstrap RPC returns a single normalized offline payload and owns relationship hydration. |
| 2026-05-22 | `staff_programme_assignments` uses scoped replace while FK-referenced reference tables keep upsert refresh. | User-scoped staff assignments have no inbound local FK dependencies and must propagate server removals; schools, programmes, years, teachers, and windows are referenced by local rows and should not be deleted during refresh. | Reference data gains tombstones or audited archive semantics. |
| 2026-05-22 | Class archive enqueues full child update payloads, not partial `{ id, class_id }` payloads. | Supabase upsert can hit an INSERT arm if the server row is missing; full payloads avoid NOT NULL failures and keep class archive sync consistent with other domain updates. | Child class removal moves to a server RPC with explicit update semantics. |
| 2026-05-25 | Add a critical hardening plan before field cutover. | Zazi's SQLite/build logs and the feature-port roadmap show a few remaining high-value Masi gaps: SQLite `busy_timeout`, sync/preload contention reduction, local-first screen completion, broader domain input hardening, and visible release/backend identity. | The hardening plan completes or a future review finds a higher-risk release blocker. |
| 2026-05-25 | Treat assignment archive/update as a lifecycle operation, not an identity operation. | Mobile users need to end their own child/class/group assignment rows during archive flows, but UPDATE must not become a way to reassign child, class, group, programme, user, creator, or original assignment timestamps. Database triggers now enforce immutable assignment identity columns. | A manager/admin mobile workflow needs reassignment; build a separate service-role/admin path. |
| 2026-05-25 | Archive outbox rows use operation-aware ordering. | Insert order can safely create access evidence early; archive order must keep access-granting assignment rows active until protected relationship cleanup rows have synced. | Sync moves to backend RPCs that apply an archive graph atomically. |
| 2026-05-25 | Assigned EAs may edit/archive assigned classes. | The app already loads assigned classes as the user's working set and exposes edit/delete UI. RLS now matches that product surface through `private.current_user_can_write_for_class(id)`. | Product decides class metadata/archive should be admin-only; then hide or disable the UI for non-created classes and tighten RLS again. |
| 2026-05-25 | App roles should not keep non-DML table privileges. | RLS does not protect `TRUNCATE`, and `REFERENCES`/`TRIGGER` are not needed by the mobile app. Public-table grants now match the intended app operations more closely. | A migration or admin feature needs user-created constraints/triggers, which should not be a mobile client concern. |
| 2026-05-26 | Maintain `documentation/rls-sync-contract-map.md` as the source of truth for RLS/sync operation contracts. | Static policy audits missed that the real contract spans repository producer fields, exact Supabase operation shape, SELECT visibility during upsert, outbox ordering, and test/probe coverage. | Any synced table, repository write path, RLS policy, migration, server payload allowlist, or outbox ordering changes. |
| 2026-05-26 | Adopt `masi-app-sqlite` as the forward backend for new app work. | Final RLS/sync fixes passed the release gate, migrations are applied to `segygjzpujphwvrubusm`, and user iPhone preview-build testing reported the new build working correctly. | User finds a blocker in broader device rollout, or the app is intentionally re-pointed to a different production Supabase project. |
| 2026-07-14 | Model reading level as mutable child state plus an immutable session snapshot. | The next session needs the latest working level, while historical sessions must preserve what the EA recorded at that time. `children.reading_level` answers the current-state question; `sessions.activities.child_reading_levels` answers the historical-event question. Changed-only child writes avoid one outbox update per unchanged attendee. | Masi replaces the single current level with a formal dated progression/history model or programme-specific levels. |
| 2026-07-14 | Defer real-data import design until the Airtable/Postgres source model is inspected with Jim. | The target SQLite/Supabase schema is not a source-to-target mapping. Inventing a normalized manifest before understanding source identifiers, relationships, history, and data quality would encode guesses into a one-time cutover tool. | Jim is ready to map the source system and place the relevant tables/data in scope. |
| 2026-07-14 | Use Sentry Cloud plus durable local diagnostic logs as complementary observability layers. | Sentry provides native and JavaScript crash capture, source-mapped releases, device/build context, offline event delivery, and alerting. Local logs remain necessary when a device cannot upload, and they supply recent breadcrumbs to remote events. Sync problems require explicit domain instrumentation because a crash SDK cannot infer that an outbox row or reconcile breaker needs attention. | Sentry becomes operationally unsuitable, or Masi adopts a centralized telemetry platform that covers native crashes and offline field diagnostics equally well. |
| 2026-07-14 | Separate pull hydration authority from relationship-absence authority. | RLS-filtered SELECTs can safely refresh rows they return, but an empty success cannot prove a row was removed. Only the no-argument, `auth.uid()`-scoped, versioned RPC snapshot may authorize local relationship end-dates. Invalid authority fails closed while ordinary hydration continues. A complete id snapshot makes reconcile safe, but a truncated content query still withholds the successful-pull stamp because safe deletion evidence is not complete replication evidence. | Pull hydration moves to one server-owned bootstrap RPC that returns both complete row content and explicit scope authority with equivalent tests and least-privilege controls. |
| 2026-07-14 | Scope same-pass outbox dependency failures to records and relationship subjects, not whole tables. | Relational order is an edge between exact rows. Table-wide propagation was safe but caused one child's failure to suppress unrelated children. Direct dependencies now compare FK to failed parent id; inverse archive dependencies compare child, class, or group subject. Missing identity mapping remains conservative. | The outbox moves to a server transaction/RPC that applies whole dependency subgraphs atomically, or a new dependency edge is added and needs an explicit identity mapping. |
| 2026-07-14 | Enable batching per exact server-operation contract, not because tables have similar columns. | Ordinary child upserts and deterministic programme-enrollment upserts can share the bounded generic batch path. Class memberships require reconcile-before-upsert, group memberships lack a settled collision path, and immutable assignment inserts require insert-or-ignore. Excluding those tables preserves correctness while removing high-volume child and enrollment round trips. | A remaining table gains a tested array operation that preserves its pre-push hooks, conflict identity, RLS visibility, and failure semantics. |
| 2026-07-14 | Treat successful SQLite bootstrap as a root application invariant. | This is a local-first app: mounting providers against an unopened or unmigrated database creates many misleading downstream failures. The root gate owns readiness, while the client owns handle disposal and resettable initialization. Retry never wipes data, and diagnostics live outside SQLite. | The app moves to a backend-owned bootstrap payload that can operate without local storage, or Expo supplies a stronger native database lifecycle boundary. |
| 2026-07-14 | Make immutable-assignment batching operation-specific. | Child, class, and group assignment inserts share deterministic identities and insert-or-ignore retry semantics, so consecutive inserts can safely use bounded array upserts. Unassignment and handover writes must update lifecycle columns on an existing row; they remain per-record and update-capable so a duplicate-ignore response cannot silently leave access active. | Supabase gains a server operation that applies assignment lifecycle changes safely in a bounded set, or field telemetry shows assignment insert batching needs a different ceiling. |
| 2026-07-14 | Derive indexes from real predicates and relationship operations, not from column inventories. | Sparse indexes are justified for the three nullable session relationships because they support equality and parent-key maintenance without indexing null-heavy rows. No current pull filters on `updated_at`; blanket timestamp indexes would tax every synced write and may be inferior to the scope-specific composite indexes the eventual delta-pull queries require. | A delta-pull query is designed, with its scope predicate and ordering known well enough to prove a matching index through query plans. |
